STAFF REPORT 
 
To:   Snyderville Basin Planning Commission   
From:   Laura Kuhrmeyer, County Planer 
Date of Meeting: June 24, 2025 
Type of Item:  Condominium Plat - Public Hearing, Possible Recommendation 
Process:  Administrative Review 
File #:   25-077 
 
 
 
Project Description 
Project Name: Warehouse Condominiums Unit C Amended 
Applicant(s):    John Davis 
Property Owner(s):   PWG Properties, LLC – C/O Jake Wolf 
Location:    4413 N Forestdale Drive, Unit C 
Zone District: Service Commercial (SC), Lower Silver Creek Soils Overlay 
Zone  
Parcel Number and Size:   PCBC-10 (2.10 acres) *Note, parcel numbers for individual 
condominium units have not yet been assigned at the time of 
publishing. 
Type of Process:   Administrative Review 
Final Land Use Authority:  County Manager 
 
Proposal 
The applicant is requesting to add a second floor within an existing condominium Unit. The 
addition will not increase the height of the building. The new level will be located between the 
existing first level and the existing roof and will add approximately 2,168 square feet to Unit C.  
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
3
 
 
Vicinity Map 
 
 
 
Background 
The Warehouse Condominiums Plat was recorded on April 7, 2025 (Exhibit D). The 
condominiums are subject to the Park City Business Center Development Agreement.  
 
The existing Low Impact Permit (see Exhibit C) for this building approved the following Uses on 
the property: 
 
1. Auto Repair, Service and Detailing 
2. General Office 
3. Restaurant, Deli or Take out intended to serve a neighborhood 
4. Retail Sales Associated with Service Commercial 
5. Warehousing and Distribution, General 
6. Storage, Vehicle 
 
4
 
 
In reviewing the Low Impact Permit, the building was found to comply with the 32’ maximum 
height requirement and all other requirements in the Snyderville Basin Development Code and 
the Park City Business Center Development Agreement. The Low Impact Permit has allocated 8 
of the parking spaces on-site to Unit C. The shell of the building has already been constructed 
and the only proposed change at this time is adding a second level within the Unit’s existing 
building envelope. No changes to the outside of the building are proposed at this time.  
 
On May 15, 2025, the Summit County Community Development Department received a 
complete application for a commercial Condominium Plat Amendment to Unit C of the 
Warehouse Condominiums. The applicant is requesting to add a level between the existing first 
level and the existing roof (i.e. not increasing height) in Unit C. This level would add 
approximately 2,168 square feet to Unit C, bringing the total gross square footage of Unit C up 
to 5,496 square feet. 
 
Analysis and Findings 
The Snyderville Basin Development Code (Code) defines Condominium as “any structure which 
has been submitted to condominium ownership under the provisions of the Utah condominium 
ownership act, whether for residential, nonresidential, and any other use”. Condominium Plats 
are recommended by the Snyderville Basin Planning Commission (SBPC) to the Summit County 
Manager after a public hearing. The SBPC may recommend that the County Manager approve, 
approve with conditions, or deny a Condominium Plat based upon written findings of fact 
according to each of the applicable standards. 
 
The Code does not contain standards for approval of a condo plat; however, Utah State Code 
requires that Condominium Plats be recorded with the associated Covenants, Conditions, and 
Restrictions for the development. These documents have been provided to Staff and are 
included as Exhibits B.  
 
Additionally, the Service Commercial (SC) Zone does not include density requirements for 
commercial uses. The unit subject to this application and all associated uses were already 
determined to comply with Chapter 4 of the Snyderville Basin Development Code (Code) which 
can be seen in Exhibit C.  
 
Staff and service providers have reviewed the application and have found compliance with all 
applicable codes. 
 
Public Notice, Meetings and Comments  
This item was noticed as a public hearing and possible recommendation for the proposed 
Warehouse Condominiums Unit C Amended, including adding a second level to Unit C, in the 
Park Record. Postcard courtesy notices were also mailed to property owners within 1,000 feet 
of the property.  
 
5
 
 
Recommendation 
Staff recommends that the Snyderville Basin Planning Commission (SBPC) review the proposed 
Warehouse Condominium Unit C Amended , conduct a public hearing and forward a positive 
recommendation to the County Manager, pursuant to the Findings of Fact, Conclusions of Law, 
and Conditions of Approval found in this Staff Report. The County Manager is the final Land Use 
Authority for Condominium Plats. 
 
Findings of Fact: 
1. The Warehouse Condominiums Plat was recorded on April 7, 2025 (Entry #1233861). 
2. The Condominiums are located within the Service Commercial (SC) Zone and the Lower 
Silver Creek Soils Overlay Zone.  
3. The Condominiums are subject to the Park City Business Center Development 
Agreement.  
4. On May 15, 2025, the Summit County Community Development Department received a 
complete application for a commercial Condominium Plat Amendment to Unit C of the 
Warehouse Condominiums, located on Parcel PCBC-10. 
5. The Unit is located at 4413 N Forestdale Dr, Park City, UT 84098.  
6. Parcel PCBC-10 is owned by PWG properties LLC. 
7. The applicant for this proposal is John Davis of Layton Davis Architects. 
8. The existing Low Impact Permit (see Exhibit C) for this building approved the following 
Uses on the property: Auto Repair, Service and Detailing; General Office; Restaurant, 
Deli or Take out intended to serve a neighborhood; Retail Sales Associated with Service 
Commercial; Warehousing and Distribution, General; and Storage, Vehicle 
9. The shell building was found to comply with the 32’ maximum height requirement and 
all other requirements in the Snyderville Basin Development Code (Code) and the Park 
City Business Center Development Agreement. 
10. The Low Impact Permit has allocated 8 of the parking spaces on-site to Unit C. 
11. The shell of the building has already been constructed and the only proposed change at 
this time is adding a level within the existing envelope. No changes to the outside of the 
building are proposed at this time.  
12. The proposed Condominium Plat Amendment includes adding a level between the 
existing first level and the existing roof (i.e. not increasing the height) in Unit C.  
13. This proposed second level would add approximately 2,168 square feet to Unit C. 
14. The Code does not contain standards for approval of a Condominium Plat; however, 
Utah State Code requires that Condominium Plats be recorded with the associated 
Covenants, Conditions, and Restrictions (CC&Rs) for the development.  
15. The CC&Rs have been provided to Staff (Exhibit B).  
16. Staff and service providers reviewed the proposal with all applicable standards and 
found compliance. 
 
Conclusions of Law:  
1. The Proposed Condominium Plat Amendment is consistent with all prior approvals for 
the project.  
6
 
 
2. Approval of the Condominium Plat Amendment will not adversely affect public health, 
safety, and welfare. 
3. The use is compatible with the existing neighborhood character and with the purpose 
provisions of the applicable zone district and will not affect surrounding land uses. 
 
Conditions of Approval: 
1. All conditions from Low Impact Permit (Exhibit C) remain in effect.  
2. Any change in use beyond what was approved in Low Impact Permit Project Number  
17-067 will require review and applicable permits from the Summit County Community 
Development Department.  
3. All necessary service provider requirements shall be noted on the final Condominium 
Plat prior to recordation.  
4. Prior to plat recordation, the Summit County Treasurer shall ensure that all taxes, tax 
notice charges, interest and penalties charged to the property have been paid.  
5. A current title report (dated within 30 days) shall be submitted to the Summit County 
Attorney’s Office prior to obtaining the Attorney’s signature on the final Condominium 
Plat. 
6. The final Condominium Plat recordation documents, including the CC&Rs, shall be in a 
form that is acceptable to the Summit County Attorney’s Office and Recorder’s Office.

FIRST AMENDMENT TO THE DECLARATION OF CONDOMINIUM  
FOR 
WAREHOUSE CONDOMINIUMS 
Park City, Summit County, Utah 
 
THIS FIRST AMENDMENT TO DECLARATION OF CONDOMINIUM FOR 
WAREHOUSE CONDOMINIUMS  (this “Amendment”) is made  effective on the date of 
recordation (the “ Effective Date ”) by Warehouse Condominiums Association, Inc.,  a Utah 
nonprofit corporation (the “Association”), on behalf of its current and future Members. 
RECITALS 
 
A. WHEREAS, PWG Properties, LLC, a Utah limited liability company (the 
“Declarant”) filed that certain Declaration of Condominium for Warehouse Condominiums, 
recorded with the Summit County Recorder on April 7, 2025, Entry No. 1233862, in Book 2855, 
starting at Page 1188 (the “Declaration”) together with that  certain Warehouse Condominiums 
plat recorded on April 7, 2025, with the Summit County Recorder as Entry No. 1233861  (the 
“Plat”); 
 
B. WHEREAS, the Declaration and Plat establish and govern that project known as 
the Warehouse Condominiums (the “ Project”), which includes six (6) individual commercial 
condominium units (the “Units”), which is further described in Exhibit A attached hereto; 
 
C. WHEREAS, as of the Effective Date, Declarant continues to own one or more Units 
within the Project; 
 
D. WHERES, the Association is a Utah nonprofit corporation formed under the Utah 
Revised Nonprofit Corporation Act, Utah Code Annotated section 16-6a-101, et seq.; 
 
E. WHEREAS, the Association is governed by the Declaration; 
 
F. WHEREAS, the Association desires to resolve a conflict between the language of 
the Declaration and Exhibit B for Common Ownership Percentage for each of the Units; 
 
G. WHEREAS, the Park City Planning Department (the “Planning Department”) has 
requested that the Declaration and Plat be amended to resolve a conflict in the square footage area 
for Unit C as it is designated on the Plat; 
 
H. WHEREAS, on even date herewith , in order to comply with the request of the 
Planning Department,  the Plat is being  amended and supplemented by that certain 
10
 
 
_________________________ recorded with the Summit County Recorder on ________ 
_____,2025 as Entry No. _______ (the “Supplemental Plat”); and 
 
I. WHEREAS, pursuant to Section s 15.2, 15.3.9,  and 15.9 of the Declaration, the 
affirmative vote of at least sixty-seven percent (67%) of the votes cast by the Owners  in addition 
to the consent of Eligible Mortgagees holding at least fifty -one percent (51%) of the undivided 
Ownership Interest in the Common Areas and Facitlities and  written consent of Declarant  is 
required for this Amendment to the Declaration or Plat and such vote and consent has been 
obtained for the recording of this Amendment with the office of the County Recorder of Summit 
County, State of Utah. 
 
NOW THEREFORE, for good and valuable consideration, the receipt and adequacy of 
which is acknowledged, the undersigned, with the required consent of Owners and Declarant, and 
on behalf of the Owners in the Project, have caused the Amendment to be prepared and recorded 
herewith, and agree that the Declaration is hereby amended as follows: 
 
AMENDMENT 
 
 
1. Defined Terms and Status of Recitals.  Capitalized terms used and not otherwise 
defined in this Amendment shall have the meaning or meanings given to them in the Declaration.  
The Recitals set forth above shall constitute a portion of the terms of this Second Amendment. 
 
2. Ownership of Common Areas and Facilities.  The first two sentences of Section 
7.1 of the Declaration shall be deleted in their entirety and replaced with the following language: 
 
7.1 Each Unit will be entitled to an undivided ownership interest in the 
Common Areas and Facilities based upon a Common Ownership Percentage which 
shall be determined by dividing the total square footage of Level 1 of each Unit by 
the total square footage of Level 1 for all Units as such square footage is shown on 
the Plat (the “Common Ownership Percentage.”)  
 
Section 7.2 of the Declaration shall be deleted in its entirety and replaced with the following 
language: 
 
7.2 The undivided interest in the Common Areas and Facilities appurtenant to 
each Unit or the Common Ownership Percentage for each Unit in the Project shall 
be as listed in Exhibit B, attached hereto.     
 
3. Exhibit B.  Exhibit B of the Declaration shall be deleted in its entirety and replaced 
with the Exhibit B attached hereto to this Amendment. 
 
4. Amended and Supplemental Plat.  The Plat shall be amended and supplemented 
by the Supplemental Plat recorded with the Summit County Recorder on ________,2025 as Entry 
No. _______, Book _______ at Pages ____ -______, attached hereto as Exhibit C and 
incorporated herein.

 SNYDERVILLE BASIN LOW IMPACT PERMIT 
 
July 14, 2023 
 
Jake Wolf

1776 Park Avenue Suite # 4

Re: Amendment to a Low Impact Permit for property located at 4413 N Forestdale Dr,  
 
Dear Mr. Wolf, 
 
This letter is to confirm that on July 17, 2023, the Summit County Community Development 
Department approved an amendment to a Low Impact Permit for a multi-use facility to allow a 
Vehicle and Equipment Sales or Rental, use on site. Staff found the application complies with 
the criteria outlined in Section 10-3-4 of the Snyderville Basin Development Code and the 
Summit (Park City) Business Park, as stated in the following Findings of Fact and Conclusions of 
Law and by satisfying the Conditions of Approval. 
 
Findings of Fact 
 
1. Parcel PCBC-10 is located at 4413 N Forestdale Dr. 
2. Parcel PCBC-10 is located entirely within the Service Commercial (SC) Zone. 
3. Parcel PCBC-10 is 2.10 acres in size. 
4. On October 23, 2020, the Community Development Department issued a low impact 
permit for a property with 2 buildings, with 5 units in building 1 and 4 units in 
building 2.  
5. The total approved footprint is 33,000 square feet. The footprint will not change as 
part of this approval.     
6. The October 23, 2020, approval allowed the following uses on the property.  
a. Auto Repair, Service and Detailing  
b. General Office 
c. Restaurant, Deli or Take out intended to serve a neighborhood. 
d. Retail Sales Associated with Service Commercial  
e. Warehousing and Distribution, General 
f. Storage, Vehicle 
7. The applicant is proposing that Vehicle and Equipment Sales or Rental be added to 
the list of uses on site.  
16
8. Vehicle and Equipment Sales or Rental is a low impact use in the Service Commercial 
Zoning district.  
9. The applicant proposes the sale and rental of high-end vehicles to customers on an 
appointment only basis.  
 
Conclusions of Law 
 
1. The plans submitted conform to standards found in the Snyderville Basin 
Development Code. 
2. The use will not significantly increase vehicular traffic in the area. 
3. The use will not increase the demand for parking on Parcel HE-B-254. 
4. The use will not increase the likelihood of pedestrian and vehicle conflict. 
5. The use will not create unsightly conditions. 
6. The use will not cause undue noise or odor levels. 
7. The use will not create dust and dirt conditions. 
8. The use will not intensify lighting conditions. 
9. The site is landscaped according to standards set forth in the Snyderville Basin 
Development Code. 
10. The use does not affect privacy for adjacent property owners. 
11. The use is generally consistent with the Snyderville Basin General Plan. 
 
Conditions of Approval 
 
1. Inventory of no more than 15 vehicles at a time in the Warehouse (units I-F) and no 
more than 6 vehicles at a time in Unit D. 
2. No exterior parking for sales inventory.  
3. The sales inventory will not force other vehicles parked inside of the facility to be 
parked outside.  
4. No exterior advertising other than typical storefront signage of the company name 
and logo. No blow-up advertising displays, promotional signs, etc.  
5. Sales hours are to be by appointment only.  
6. Sales of food on site are for members of the use and guests only. No sales to outside 
individuals is allowed.  
 
This Low Impact Permit is valid for a period of one (1) year from the date of approval and will 
expire if substantial construction or development has not taken place or continued in good 
faith, without interruption. Please be aware that this approval may be appealed to the Summit 
County Council within ten (10) calendar days from the date of the decision. 
 
If you have any questions, please feel free to contact me at (435) 336-3118 or by email, 
rmilliner@summitcounty.org. 
 
Sincerely, 
 
17
 
 
 
Ray Milliner 
County Planner

STAFF REPORT 
 
To:   Snyderville Basin Planning Commission   
From:   Laura Kuhrmeyer, County Planer 
Date of Meeting: June 24, 2025 
Type of Item:  Condominium Plat - Public Hearing, Possible Recommendation 
Process:  Administrative Review 
File #:   25-078 
 
 
 
Project Description  
Project Name: Park East II Business Community Condominiums Unit 42 
Amended 
Applicant(s):    Steve Stanton 
Property Owner(s):   Steven S Stanton Separate Property Trust 
Location:    4554 N Forestdale Drive #42 
Zone District: Service Commercial (SC), Lower Silver Creek Soils Overlay 
Zone  
Parcel Number and Size:   PEIIBCC-42-2AM (0.21 acres) 
Type of Process:   Administrative Review 
Final Land Use Authority:  County Manager 
 
Proposal 
The applicant is requesting to add a second floor within an existing shell building. The addition 
will not increase the height of the building. The new level will be located between the existing 
first level and the existing roof and will add approximately 9,000 square feet to Unit 42.  
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
23
2 
 
Vicinity Map 
 
 
 
Background 
The Park East II Business Community Condominiums Unit 41 Amended, which created Unit 42, 
was recorded on November 22, 2021. The condominiums are subject to a Parking Management 
Plan approved by the Community Development Director and recorded with the County. The 
revised Parking Management Plan was recorded on December 19, 2023 and is attached as 
Exhibit D. 
 
The Parking Management Plan identifies nine (9) reserved parking spaces and one (1) overhead 
door parking space for Unit 42. The existing approved Low Impact Permit (Project Number  
23-024) for this Unit allowed for “Automotive Repair, Service, and Detailing” within this Unit. 
There is no proposed change of use for the unit at this time. 
 
The buildings were found to comply with the 32’ maximum height requirement and all other 
requirements in the Snyderville Basin Development Code (Code) and the Park City Business 
Center Development Agreement. The shell of the building has already been constructed and 
the only proposed change at this time is adding a level within the existing envelope. No changes 
to the outside of the building are proposed at this time.  
 
24
3 
 
On May 20, 2025, the Summit County Community Development Department received a 
complete application for a commercial Condominium Plat Amendment to Unit 42 of the Park 
East II Business Community Condominiums. The applicant has requested to add a level between 
the existing first level and the existing roof (i.e. not increasing height) in Unit 42. This level 
would add approximately 9,000 square feet to Unit 42, bringing the total up to 18,000 gross 
square feet. 
 
Analysis and Findings 
The Snyderville Basin Development Code (Code) defines Condominium as “any structure which 
has been submitted to condominium ownership under the provisions of the Utah condominium 
ownership act, whether for residential, nonresidential, and any other use”. Condominium Plats 
are recommended by the Snyderville Basin Planning Commission (SBPC) to the Summit County 
Manager after a public hearing. The SBPC may recommend that the County Manager approve, 
approve with conditions, or deny a Condominium Plat based upon written findings of fact 
according to each of the applicable standards. 
 
The Code does not contain standards for approval of a condo plat; however, Utah State Code 
requires that Condominium Plats be recorded with the associated Covenants, Conditions, and 
Restrictions for the development. These documents have been provided to Staff and are 
included as Exhibit B.  
 
Additionally, the Service Commercial (SC) Zone does not include density requirements for 
commercial uses. The unit subject to this application and all associated uses were already 
determined to comply with Chapter 4 of the Snyderville Basin Development Code (Code) which 
can be seen in Exhibit C.  
 
Staff and service providers have reviewed the application and have found compliance with all 
applicable codes. 
 
Public Notice, Meetings and Comments   
This item was noticed as a public hearing and possible recommendation for the proposed 
Warehouse Condominiums Unit C Amended, including adding a second level to Unit C, in the 
Park Record. Postcard courtesy notices were also mailed to property owners within 1,000 feet 
of the property.  
 
Recommendation 
Staff recommends that the Snyderville Basin Planning Commission (SBPC) review the proposed 
Condominium Plat Amendment, conduct a public hearing and forward a positive 
recommendation to the County Manager, pursuant to the Findings of Fact, Conclusions of Law, 
and Conditions of Approval found in this Staff Report. The County Manager is the final Land Use 
Authority for Condominium Plats. 
 
25
4 
 
Findings of Fact: 
1. On May 20, 2025, the Summit County Community Development Department received a 
complete application for a commercial Condominium Plat Amendment to Unit 42 of the 
Park East II Business Community Condominiums, Parcel PEIIIBCC-42-2AM 
2. The Unit is located at 4554 N Forestdale Dr, Park City, UT 84098. The property is within 
the Service Commercial (SC) Zone and the Lower Silver Creek Soils Overlay Zone.  
3. The Park East II Business Community Condominiums Unit 41 Amended, which created 
Unit 42, was recorded on November 22, 2021.  
4. The condominiums are subject to a Parking Management Plan approved by the 
Community Development Director and recorded with the County.  
a. The revised Parking Management Plan was recorded on December 19, 2023 and 
is attached as Exhibit D. 
5. The property is owned by Steven S Stanton Separate Property Trust.  
6. The applicant for this proposal is Steve Stanton.  
7. The property is subject to the Park City Business Center Development Agreement.  
8. The existing approved Low Impact Permit (Project #23-024) for Unit 42 allows for 
“Automotive Repair, Service, and Detailing” within this Unit. There is no proposed 
change of use for the Unit at this time. 
9. The building was found to comply with the 32’ maximum height requirement and all 
other requirements in the Snyderville Basin Development Code (Code) and the Park City 
Business Center Development Agreement. 
10. The shell of the building has already been constructed and the only proposed change at 
this time is adding a level within the existing envelope. No changes to the outside of the 
building are proposed at this time.  
11. The proposed Condominium Plat Amendment includes adding a level between the 
existing first level and the existing roof (i.e. not increasing the height) in Unit 42.  
a. This level would add approximately 9,000 square feet to Unit 42, bringing the 
total gross square footage up to 18,000 square feet. 
12. The Code does not contain standards for approval of a condominium plat; however, 
Utah State Code requires that Condominium Plats be recorded with the associated 
Covenants, Conditions, and Restrictions (CC&Rs) for the development.  
13. The CC&Rs have been provided to Staff (Exhibit B).  
14. Staff and service providers reviewed the proposal with all applicable standards. 
 
Conclusions of Law:  
1. The Proposed Condominium Plat Amendment is consistent with all prior approvals for 
the project.  
2. Approval of the Condominium Plat Amendment will not adversely affect public health, 
safety, and welfare. 
3. The use is compatible with the existing neighborhood character and with the purpose 
provisions of the applicable zone district and will not affect surrounding land uses. 
 
Conditions of Approval: 
26
5 
 
1. All conditions from Low Impact Permit Project Number 23-024 remain in effect.  
2. Any change in use beyond what was approved in Low Impact Permit Project Number  
23-024 will require review and applicable permits from the Summit County Community 
Development Department.  
3. All necessary service provider requirements shall be noted on the final condominium 
plat prior to recordation.  
4. Prior to plat recordation, the Summit County Treasurer shall ensure that all taxes, tax 
notice charges, interest and penalties charged to the property have been paid.  
5. A current title report (dated within 30 days) shall be submitted to the Summit County 
Attorney’s Office prior to obtaining the Attorney’s signature on the final condominium 
plat. 
6. The final condominium plat recordation documents, including the CC&Rs, shall be in a 
form that is acceptable to the Summit County Attorney’s Office and Recorder’s Office.

THIS DECLARATION OF COVENANTS, CONDITIONS AND RESTRICTIONS forPARK
EAST II(the"Declaration")ismade and executedby PCBC LOT 19,LLC, a Utah Limited
LiabilityCompany (the"Declarant")this?T day of cem0. , 2017 pursuant to the
provisionsofTitle57,Chapter8,Utah Code Annotated,asamended.
RECITALS
A. Declarantistheowner of certainrealpropertylocatedin Summit County, Utah, more
particularlydescribedon ExhibitA attachedheretoand incorporatedhereinby reference
(the"Land").
B. Declarantintendstoconstruct7 buildingson theLand (the"Project")thatwillinclude48
condominium Units,and certainCommon Areas and Facilitiesand Limited Common
Areas, and desiresto provideforpreservationof thevaluesand improvements inthe
project,and forthemaintenanceofthecommon areasand common facilities.To thisend,
and forthebenefitofthepropertyand theowners thereof,Declarantdesirestosubjectthe
propertytotheeasements,covenants,conditions,restrictions,chargesand lienssetforth
intheDeclaration.
C. The Projectisknown asParkEastII.
D. The projectisnotacooperative.
E. Declarantcausedtobe prepareda platfortheprojectunder thename ofPark EastII,in
Summit County,Utah,which describesthesubdivisionoftheLand and Projectintounits
and common areas(hereindescribedasthe"Plat").
F. ThisDeclarationismade and recordedagainsttheLand, intendedtosubjecttheLand to
thisDeclarationand theprovisionsoftheUtah Condominium Ownership Act,Title57,
Chapter8,Utah Code Annotated,asamended (the"Act").
1. DEFINITIONS
Unlessthecontextclearlyindicatesotherwise,certaintermsasused inthisDeclaration
shallhave themeaning setforthinthisSection1.

Code Annotated,asamended.

nonprofitcorporation,organized for the purposes setforthin thisDeclaration,as

31
amended.

affairsof theAssociationunder theprovisionsof Section11 below. In addition,the
Board shallbe deemed tobe the"Management Committee" asdescribedintheAct.

ordinarycourseofsuchperson'sbusiness,constructabuildingofUnitsintheProject.

timetotime.The Bylaws arelocatedinSection11hereof.

2. SUBMISSION OF THE PROJECT TO THE ACT
2.1. The Declarant,astheowner oftheLand, hereby submitstheLand, theBuildings

3. IMPROVEMENTS
3.1. The ProjectshallcontainsevenBuildingswithdesignatedparkingand forty-eight
(48)Condominium Units.The principalmaterialsfrom which theBuildingwillbe
constructedare concrete footingsand foundation;wood framing; steeland
concrete;cement fiberboard siding;sheetrockinteriorsand steelroof;and such
other materialsas allowed by currentbuildingcodes. The Building willbe
suppliedwith electricity,naturalgas,water and sewer service.The Projectand
surroundingareaalsoconsistsofCommon Areas and Facilities,including,butnot
limitedto,roadways, parking,walkways, landscapingand Limited Common
Areas.

11.1. Board of Directors.The Associationshallbe managed by a Board of Directors
which shallinitiallybe comprised ofthree(3)persons;provided,however, until
theexpirationoftheTransitionEvents,theDeclarantshallhave theexclusiveand

Associationmeeting aftertheoccurrenceoftheTransitionEvents,theMembers
oftheBoard ofDirectorsshallbe electedby theOwners. To providecontinuityof

electedfora three(3)yearterm;one (1)fora two (2)yearterm and one (1)fora
one (1)yearterm.The Members oftheBoard ofDirectorsshallalsoconstitutethe
Board ofDirectorsoftheAssociation.

of Directorsmust be an individualOwner ortherepresentativeof Declarant,as
providedinSection11.1above.

oftheTransitionEventsmay be filledby theaffirmativevoteofa majorityofthe
Members oftheAssociationata regularorspecialmeeting. A directorelectedto
filla vacancy shallholdofficefortheremainderofthedirector'sunexpiredterm
which gave riseto the vacancy and untilhis or her successoriselectedand
qualified,oruntilhisorherearlierdismissal,death,resignationorremoval.

failson three(3) successiveoccasionsto attendBoard of Directorsmeetings
(whetherregularorspecial)orwho has failedtoattendatleasttwenty-five(25%)
of allBoard of Directormeetings (whetherregularor special)held duringany
twelve(12)month periodshallautomaticallyforfeithisorher seatand shallbe
deemed tohave been dismissedfrom theBoard ofDirectors.
11.5. Removal ofBoard ofDirectorsMembers/Declarant'sRights.Except forBoard of
Directorsmembers appointedby the Declarantbefore the occurrence of the
TransitionEvents,Board ofDirectorsmembers may be removed atany time by
the affirmativevote of a majority of the Members of the Association.A
replacementtoservetheremainderoftheremoved director'sunexpiredterm shall
be electedatthesame meeting.
11.6. Term. Unless a directorforfeitsorotherwiselosessuch director'sseatasherein
provided,a directorshallholdofficefortheterm thathe or she was electedand
untilhis or her successoriselectedand qualified,or untilhis or her earlier
dismissal,death,resignationorremoval.

13.DESTRUCTION, CONDEMNATION, OBSOLESCENCE
13.1. Destruction,Condemnation, and Obsolescence.The followingprovisionsshall
apply with respectto the destruction,condemnation, or obsolescenceof the
Project.
13.2. Definitions.Each ofthefollowingtermsshallhave themeaning indicated:
13.2.1. "SubstantialDestruction"shallexistwhenever, as a resultof any
damage or destructiontotheProjector any partthereof,theexcessof
theestimatedcostofrestorationoverthefundsavailableisTwenty-Five
percent(25%) ormore oftheestimatedrestoredvalueoftheProject.
-36-
66
13.2.2. "PartialDestruction"shallmean any otherdamage ordestructiontothe
Projectorany partthereof.
13.2.3. "SubstantialCondemnation" shallexistwhenever a complete takingof
theProjectora takingofpartoftheProjecthas occurredunder eminent
domain or by grantor conveyance in lieuof condemnation, and the
excessof the estimatedcostof restorationover the funds availableis
Twenty Five (25%) percentor more of theestimatedrestoredvalueof
theProject.
13.2.4. "PartialCondemnation" shallmean any othersuch taken by eminent
domain orgrantorconveyance inlieuthereof.
13.2.5. "SubstantialObsolescence"shallexistwhenever theProjectorany part
thereofhas reached such a stateof obsolescenceor disrepairthatthe
excessof the estimatedcostof restorationover the funds availableis
Twenty Five (25%) percentormore of theestimatedrestoredvalueof
theProject.
13.2.6. "PartialObsolescence"shallmean any stateofobsolescenceordisrepair
which doesnotconstituteSubstantialObsolescence.
13.2.7. "RestoredValue" shallmean thefairmarket value of theProjectafter
Restorationasdeterminedby anMAI orotherqualifiedappraisal.
13.2.8. "Estimated Cost of Restoration"shallmean the estimatedcosts of
restoringtheProjecttoitsformercondition.
13.2.9. "AvailableFunds" shallmean any proceedsofinsurance,condemnation
awards,payments inlieuofcondemnation,and any uncommitted funds
of the Board of Directorsor insuranceproceeds legallyrequiredtobe
paidtoany partyotherthantheAssociation,includinga mortgagee,or
that portion of any condemnation award or payment in lieu of
condemnation payabletotheOwner orMortgagee forthecondemnation
ortakingoftheUnitinwhich theyareinterested.
13.3. Determinationby Board of Directors.Upon the occurrenceof any damage or
destructiontotheProjectorany partthereof,orupon a completeorpartialtaking
oftheProjectundereminentdomain orby grantorconveyance inlieuthereof,the
Board of Directorsshallmake a determinationas to whether the excess of
EstimatedCostsofRestorationoverAvailableFunds istwenty-five(25%) percent
ormore oftheestimatedRestoredValue oftheProjectInaddition,theBoard of
Directorsshall,from time to time, review the conditionof the Projectto
determine whether Substantial Obsolescence exists. In making such
determinationsthe Board of Directorsmay retainand relyupon one or more
qualifiedappraisersorotherprofessionals.
13.4. RestorationoftheProject.RestorationoftheProjectshallbe undertakenby the
Board ofDirectorspromptlywithouta voteoftheOwners intheeventofPartial
Destruction,PartialCondemnation, or PartialObsolescence and shallalso be

67
undertakenintheeventofSubstantialDestruction,SubstantialCondemnation, or

by Owners collectivelyholdingatleastsixty-seven(67%) percentoftheProject's

 SNYDERVILLE BASIN LOW IMPACT PERMIT 
 
May 31, 2023 
 
Michael Barton 
2005 E 2700 S 
Salt Lake City, UT 84109   via email: michael@laytondavisarchitects.com 
 
CC: Steven Stanton    via email: steve@stantonarchitects.com  
 
Re: Low Impact Permit for property located at 4554 North Forestdale Dr. Unit 42, Parcel: 
PEIIBCC-42-2AM; File #23-024 
 
Mr. Barton, 
 
This letter is to confirm that on May 31, 2023, the Summit County Community 
Development Department approved a Low Impact Permit for the proposed Tire Storage 
and Replacement and General Office, as identified on the attached, approved plan. Staff 
found the application complies with the criteria outlined in Section 10-3-4 of the 
Snyderville Basin Development Code, as articulated in the following Findings of Fact and 
Conclusions of Law and by satisfying the Conditions of Approval. 
 
Findings of Fact: 
1. Parcel PEIIBCC-42-2AM is located at 4554 North Forestdale Dr. Unit 42. 
2. Parcel PEIIBCC-42-2AM is located wholly within the Service Commercial Zoning 
District. 
3. The applicant proposes to change the use of Unit 42 from “warehousing and 
distribution” to “Automotive Repair, Service, and Detailing”. 
4. “Automotive Repair, Service, and Detailing” is listed as a Low Impact Use in the 
Service Commercial zone.  
5. The construction of the “Automotive Repair, Service, and Detailing” is a tenant 
improvement for space within the existing structure. 
6. The applicant has provided an updated proposed parking management plan which 
identifies ten (10) reserved parking spaces for Unit 42.  
7. No on street parking is proposed at this site. 
8. No exterior façade changes are proposed as part of this application. 
9. Staff has reviewed the application for compliance with the standards as found in 
the Park City Business Center Development Agreement Book of Exhibits and finds 
no conflicts. 
10. Staff has reviewed the application for compliance with standards found in Chapter 
4 of the Snyderville Basin Development Code and finds no conflicts. 
95
 
Conclusions of Law: 
1. The plans submitted conform to standards found in the Snyderville Basin 
Development Code, including but not limited to 10-3-4 and 10-4. 
2. The plans submitted conform to standards found in the Park City Business Center 
Development Agreement and Book of Exhibits. 
 
Conditions of Approval: 
1. This approval is for Unit 42 only. This does not serve as any implied or direct 
approval of any other unit within the Park East II Condominium project.  
2. All necessary permits must be obtained, and fees shall be paid, prior to the 
commencement of any construction activity, including but not limited to the Summit 
County Health, Summit County Engineering and the Summit County Building 
Departments. 
3. The Parking Management Plan shall be approved by the Community Development 
Director and recorded at the Summit County Recorder’s Office prior to Certificate 
of Occupancy issuance. 
4. No modification to the structure or change of use authorized under the approved 
Low Impact Permit is permitted without the prior written approval of the Summit 
County Community Development Department.  
5. Any change in use may require additional permit(s) through the Planning 
Department. 
6. There are no Setback exceptions listed in the Development Code for the Service 
Commercial zone, so all structures (including mechanical equipment) must meet 
the required Setbacks. 
7. Final confirmation of the design, materials, and colors shall be confirmed and 
approved by Staff during the Building Permit review. 
8. All exterior light fixtures shall be full cut off, directed downward, and shall comply 
with 11-6-20. 
9. All landscaping shall be compliant with the Park City Business Center 
Development Agreement and the Snyderville Basin Development Code. 
10. All service provider requirements must be met prior to issuance of a building 
permit. 
11. Removal of and disturbance of existing vegetation shall be minimized. Native 
vegetation shall be used to reduce the impact of development on steep slopes and 
ridgelines. 
 
This Low Impact Permit is valid for a period of one (1) year from the date of approval and 
will expire if substantial construction or development has not taken place or continued in 
good faith, without interruption. Failure to meet the aforementioned conditions may result 
in the revocation of this permit. Please be aware that this approval may be appealed to 
the Summit County Council within ten (10) calendar days from the date of the decision. 
 
If you have any questions, please feel free to contact me at (435) 336-3134 or by email, 
lkuhrmeyer@summitcounty.org.  
 
96
Sincerely,       Approved by: 
 
 
 
Laura Kuhrmeyer      Patrick J. Putt 
County Planner      Community Development Director

5. Enforcement. Each Unit isresponsibletoensureitsemployees and visitorsonlypark in
thatUnit'sOverhead Door Space orReserved Space oran availableShared Space and do
notaccidentallypark inanotherUnit'sOverhead Door Space,Reserved Space,orSpecial
Reserved Space. IfaUnit'semployees orvisitorsviolatetheserules,anyone can register

model ofthevehicle,thelicenseplatenumber, and theday and time oftheparking
violation.

EastIII,whichever thecasemay be,and reportthisinformationtoSummit County by
March 1ofeach year.Intheeventthatthetotalnumber ofemployees working each shift
forallofPark EastIIand Park EastIIIexceedsthetotalnumber ofparkingspaces,
Summit County may takereasonableenforcement action,includingsuspendingissuance
offuturebuildingpermits.

7. Rights. The assignmentofparkingspacestoUnitsdoes notconstituteatransferof

8. Amendment. The Associationsmay, withthewrittenconsentoftheSummit County
Community Development Director,reviseoramend thisPlan from time totimeto
addressparkingneeds atPark EastIIand Park EastIII.An amendment ofthisPlan shall
be effectiveupon theAssociationsrecordinga copy intheofficeoftheSummit County
Recorder and providinga copy toallUnitOwners and Summit County.
9. Ride Sharing, The Associationsencouragesridesharingand use ofbicyclestoreduce
strainon limitedparkingspacesatPark EastIIand Park EastIII.Ride sharingisalsoa
more environmentallyfriendlyand sustainableway tocommute asitreducesair
pollution,carbon emissions,fuelconsumption, and trafficcongestion.
10.Shared Parking. The AssociationencouragesUnitstoconsidersharedparking
arrangementswith one anothertotakeadvantageofutilizationpatternsthanmight allow
theuse ofaReserved Space toservetwo ormore Unitswithoutconflict.For example, a
Unitthatmay notbe usingitsReserved Space may allowanotherUnit'semployees or
visitorstouse it.
11.No On-Street Parking; Signage.Parkingon thestreetinPark EastIIand Park EastIII
outsideofdesignatedand properlymarked parkingspacesisnotpermitted.The
Associationswillinstallsignageprohibitingindicating"No On-StreetParking".

theAssociationoritsprofessionalpropertymanager, Blooming PropertyManagement &

orany successorprofessionalpropertymanager duly appointedby theAssociations.

the"management company" herein).Blooming willsupplypersonneltoconduct
periodicrandom observationsand compliance audits.Blooming manages both
Associationsoperationsand has accesstoallUnit owner and tenantcontactinformation.

STAFF REPORT 
 
 
To:   Snyderville Basin Planning Commission   
From:   Amir Caus, AICP, Senior Planner 
Date of Meeting: June 24, 2025  
Type of Item:   Low Impact Permit - Public Hearing, Possible Action  
Process:   Administrative Review 
File:   25-070 
 
 
Proposal 
 
The applicant is proposing to construct a 10,945 sq. ft. retail/office building on Lot 6 of the 
Quarry Junction at Pinebrook Subdivision. The applicant is proposing a shell building and the 
tenants have not yet been determined.  
 
The applicant has stated that the building has been designed to “accommodate a flexible mix of 
professional office and retail tenants. It is not configured to support restaurants, aligning with 
both parking capacity and community vision. Our intent is to attract good-credit tenants that 
provide useful services to the surrounding Pinebrook, Summit Park, and Jeremy Ranch 
neighborhoods, augmenting a cohesive and vibrant local center.” 
 
 
Project Description 
 
Project Name: Quarry Village Lot 6 Commercial Shell Building Low Impact Permit  
Applicant(s):   Michael Anderson, CORE Architecture 
Property Owner(s):   Clover Farms LLC (Christian Vaccari) 
Location:   8211 Gorgoza Pines Road, Pinebrook, Summit County, UT  
Zone District:   Rural Residential (RR) – also governed by Gorgoza Pines Consent  
    Agreement and Quarry Junction at Pinebrook Plat A 
Parcel Number and Size: QJPB-A-6-1AM (0.45 acres)  
Type of Process:   Administrative 
Final Land Use Authority: Community Development Director  
 
 
 
126
2 
 
 
Vicinity Map 
 
 
 
Background 
 
The Quarry Junction at Pinebrook Subdivision was approved on May 21, 2004 and consists of 
eleven (11) lots. The subdivision was created pursuant to the Gorgoza Pines Ranch Consent 
Agreement, which was approved by the Summit County Board of Commissioners 
on February 24, 1997. The agreement was the result of a vested rights suit between Summit 
County and the developers of Gorgoza Pines Ranch. 
 
The subdivision was approved for ~170,000 square feet of commercial density. Several of the 
buildings have been constructed, or the lots have been subdivided and entitled 
with maximum square footage allocations. 
 
In 2003, as part of a Plat Amendment process to reconfigure the Quarry Junction Subdivision, 
the Snyderville Basin Planning Commission conditioned that all buildings in the subdivision be 
processed through the Low Impact Permit process and that the Snyderville Basin Planning 
Commission make a recommendation on architectural and landscape plans to the Community 
Development Director prior to any final approval.  
 
 
 
127
3 
 
 
Analysis and Findings 
 
The Quarry Junction at Pinebrook Subdivision is governed by the Gorgoza Pines Consent 
Agreement which lists the type of uses allowed. The applicant has stated the proposed uses will 
be consistent with the Consent Agreement. 
 
The Quarry Junction at Pinebrook Subdivision designates maximum gross square footage and 
maximum gross building footprints for each lot. Lot 6 is allowed 11,144 sq. ft. of gross 
building area and 5,572 sq. ft. of gross building footprint. As designed, the proposed building is 
in compliance with the square footage allotments. 
 
Consent Agreement Compliance 
 
The following development standards are identified in the Consent Agreement and are 
applicable to Lot 6. 
 
Densities and Uses: The uses and densities… shall be in accordance with the Consent 
Agreement. Parcels A and B as shown in the Consent Agreement are intended for a variety of 
retail commercial, service commercial, office uses or single or multiple family dwelling units.  
 
Analysis: Business licenses for future uses will be issued pursuant to the allowed uses 
identified in the Consent Agreement. The square footage limitations identified on the 
subdivision plat have been complied with. COMPLIES AS CONDITIONED 
 
Setbacks: To the extent that standard setbacks cannot be met within a development, by virtue 
of such approved densities and uses, such setback requirements shall be waived or relaxed as 
long as the County reasonably determines that public safety, health, and welfare concerns can 
be adequately satisfied.  
 
Analysis: The required setbacks in the RR zone are thirty feet from the edge of the right-
of-way, fifty five (55) feet from the centerline of the road (where property line extends 
to the road) and twelve (12) feet from the side and rear property lines. The proposed 
approximate setbacks are: 
LOT 6  
Front Side Side Rear 
~41’ from 
centerline of 
Quarry Road 
(North side) 
~47’ from 
centerline of 
Gorgoza Pines 
Road 
(East side) 
10’ 
(West side) 
40’ 
(North side) 
 
The Quarry Village project was a master planned project and all infrastructure, including 
parking, were improved when the project was approved. The project accommodated 
compact parking areas and circulation throughout. The proposed setbacks are similar to  
 
128
4 
 
 
the existing Quarry Village buildings and follow the master planned pattern. Service 
providers, including the Park City Fire Department and Summit County Engineering 
Department have reviewed the proposal and have not found any concerns with public 
safety, health, and welfare. COMPLIES AS CONDITIONED 
 
Landscaping: Landscaping and maintenance requirements shall be in accordance with Summit 
County ordinances.  
 
Analysis: The applicant is proposing landscaping that includes trees, shrubs, and 
perennials. COMPLIES 
 
Parking: Consideration should be given to limiting such parking surface area to the extent 
reasonably possible. Solutions such as on street parking and joint use parking facilities shall be 
considered.  
 
Analysis: Summit County employs a maximum parking requirement rather than a 
minimum for commercial space. Quarry Village has historically used shared parking. 
Staff historically has not recorded any complaints regarding parking conflicts. The 
parking being provided is considered adequate. COMPLIES 
 
Plazas: A number of smaller plazas… are encouraged for each specific development as it occurs. 
This could include play areas, sitting areas, or simply pleasant areas to congregate.  
Analysis: The applicant has stated; “A green space plaza is featured between the lot 6 
building and the adjacent Kodiak Cakes building providing seating, landscaping, and 
community connectivity. An open space plaza is also provided where sidewalks intersect 
near the roundabout allowing for outdoor interactions while enhancing the pedestrian 
experience and supporting indoor/outdoor engagement.” COMPLIES 
Scale: Commercial development within the Project shall be of a scale that is typical of a 
traditional town. There may be locations for anchor tenants, together with significant space for 
smaller scale users.  
Analysis: The anchor tenant in the overall Quarry Village project is the existing grocery 
store. The applicant has stated that; “The building design features elements that 
complement the surrounding buildings with their associated scale and character 
including sloped roof features to provide both wayfinding for building entry and provide 
some shelter from the elements. Exterior finishes include simulated wood siding, metal 
accents, standing seam metal roofing, and stucco with strategically located control 
joints. Large storefront glazed openings engage an open space plaza provided where 
sidewalks intersect near the roundabout to allow for indoor/outdoor interactions 
enhancing the pedestrian experience and allowing for additional building engagement.” 
The intent of the proposed buildings is to provide spaces to accommodate retail and 
office, which are uses allowed in the Agreement. The applicant’s plans show sidewalk  
129
5 
 
 
connections to the existing infrastructure along Quarry Road, Gorgoza Pine Road, and 
Lot 10. COMPLIES AS CONDITIONED 
Building Heights: Generally building heights will be restricted to two (2) stories. A third story is 
allowed so long as it occurs within the area formed by a pitched roof on the two (2) story 
structure where approved by Summit County. The Gorgoza Pines Consent Agreement allows 
heights to be up to 35 feet above natural or finished grade, whichever is lower.  
 
Analysis: The building has been designed as a two-story structure with a ~32-foot 
height. As drawn, the applicant is below the 35-foot maximum height allowed. 
COMPLIES 
 
Signage and Lighting: Outdoor signs and lighting shall be in accordance with Summit County 
ordinances.  
 
Analysis: The applicant is proposing wall packs at each entrance. The wall packs are 
directed downward and are of full cutoff variety. The applicant is also utilizing existing 
parking lot lighting. The applicant has stated that no other lighting is anticipated. Staff 
has included conditions of approval to limit the temperature and brightness of each 
exterior fixture. COMPLIES AS CONDITIONED 
 
Architecture: Buildings should be designed to invite public comprehension and reflect their 
function or use. Access, when necessary, should be easily discernible and obvious without the 
necessity of excessive signage. Facades should be scaled to and designed to accommodate use 
by humans. Vehicular and equipment structure design should mask the scale of their use. 
Decorative elements may be used to enhance, but should not suggest cosmetic treatment.  
 
Analysis: The applicant has stated that; “The building design features elements that 
complement the surrounding buildings with their associated scale and character 
including sloped roof features to provide both wayfinding for building entry and provide 
some shelter from the elements. Exterior finishes include simulated wood siding, metal 
accents, standing seam metal roofing, and stucco with strategically located control 
joints. Large storefront glazed openings engage an open space plaza provided where 
sidewalks intersect near the roundabout to allow for indoor/outdoor interactions 
enhancing the pedestrian experience and allowing for additional building engagement.” 
 
All mechanical is being clustered on the West portion of the structure and is being 
screened via a tall parapet. The applicant has confirmed that there will not be any 
façade mechanical equipment. Staff have included a condition of approval that requires 
all mechanical equipment to be screened, including future tenant improvements. Staff 
have also included conditions of approval that prohibits façade mechanical equipment 
and that if any is proposed that a Low Impact Permit Amendment would be required.

Recommendation 
 
Staff recommends that the Snyderville Basin Planning Commission review the proposed Low 
Impact Permit, conduct a public hearing, and make a positive recommendation to the 
Community Development Director to approve the application, pursuant to the following 
Findings of Fact, Conclusions of Law, and Conditions of Approval: 
 
Findings of Fact: 
 
1. The Quarry Junction at Pinebrook Subdivision was approved on May 21, 2004 and 
consists of eleven (11) lots.  
2. The Quarry Junction at Pinebrook Subdivision was created pursuant to the Gorgoza 
Pines Ranch Consent Agreement, which was approved by the Summit County Board of 
Commissioners on February 24, 1997.  
3. The Gorgoza Pines Ranch Consent Agreement was the result of a vested rights suit 
between Summit County and the developers of Gorgoza Pines Ranch. 
133
9 
 
4. The Quarry Junction at Pinebrook Subdivision was approved for ~170,000 square feet of 
commercial density.  
5. Several of the buildings have been constructed, or the lots have been subdivided and 
entitled with maximum square footage allocations. 
6. In 2003, as part of a Plat Amendment process to reconfigure the Quarry Junction 
Subdivision, the Snyderville Basin Planning Commission conditioned that all buildings in 
the subdivision be processed through the Low Impact Permit process and that the 
Snyderville Basin Planning Commission make a recommendation on architectural and 
landscape plans to the Community Development Director prior to any final approval.  
7. Lot 6 of the Quarry Junction at Pinebrook Subdivision is located at 8211 Gorgoza Pines 
Road (Parcel QJPB-A-6-1AM - 0.45 acres), Pinebrook, Summit County, UT  
8. Per the Quarry Junction at Pinebrook Subdivision Lot 6 is entitled to 11,144 sq. ft. of 
gross building area and 5,572 sq. ft. of gross building footprint. 
9. The applicant is proposing to construct a 10,945 sq. ft. retail/office building on Lot 6 of 
the Quarry Junction at Pinebrook Subdivision.  
10. The applicant is proposing a shell building and the tenants have not yet been 
determined.  
11. Business licenses for future uses will be issued pursuant to the allowed uses identified in 
the Gorgoza Pines Ranch Consent Agreement. 
12. Clover Farms LLC (Christian Vaccari) is the owner of Parcel QJPB-A-6-1AM and has given 
consent to process the proposed Low Impact Permit. 
13. Per the Gorgoza Pines Ranch Consent Agreement: To the extent that standard setbacks 
cannot be met within a development, by virtue of such approved densities and uses, such 
setback requirements shall be waived or relaxed as long as the County reasonably 
determines that public safety, health, and welfare concerns can be adequately satisfied.  
14. Service providers, including the Park City Fire Department and Summit County 
Engineering Department have reviewed the proposal and have not found any concerns 
that the proposed setback reductions will negatively impact public safety, health, and 
welfare.  
15. Per the Gorgoza Pines Ranch Consent Agreement: Landscaping and maintenance 
requirements shall be in accordance with Summit County ordinances.  
16. The applicant is proposing landscaping that includes trees, shrubs, and perennials.  
17. Per the Gorgoza Pines Ranch Consent Agreement: Consideration should be given to 
limiting such parking surface area to the extent reasonably possible. Solutions such as on 
street parking and joint use parking facilities shall be considered.  
18. Quarry Village has historically used shared parking.  
19. Staff historically has not recorded any complaints regarding parking conflicts.  
20. Snow storage areas have been identified on the site plan.  
21. The parking lots in the development are intended to be shared among the various 
tenants and snow removal occurs throughout the development.  
22. The roads and parking lots are private and will be maintained by the existing 
Homeowner’s Association, which includes the removal of snow.  
23. Per the Gorgoza Pines Ranch Consent Agreement: Commercial development within the 
Project shall be of a scale that is typical of a traditional town. There may be locations for 
anchor tenants, together with significant space for smaller scale users.  
134
10 
 
24. The applicant stated the intent of the proposed buildings is to provide spaces to 
accommodate retail and office, which are uses allowed in the Agreement.  
25. The Gorgoza Pines Consent Agreement allows heights to be up to 35 feet above natural 
or finished grade, whichever is lower.  
26. The building has been designed as a two-story structure with a ~32-foot height.  
27. Per the Gorgoza Pines Ranch Consent Agreement: Outdoor signs and lighting shall be in 
accordance with Summit County ordinances.  
28. The applicant is proposing wall packs at each entrance.  
29. The wall packs are directed downward and are of full cutoff variety.  
30. The applicant is also utilizing existing parking lot lighting.  
31. Due to the building location and topography, the building has two front facades which 
allow the project an opportunity for a human-scale design.  
32. The applicant has stated that; “The building design features elements that complement 
the surrounding buildings with their associated scale and character including sloped roof 
features to provide both wayfinding for building entry and provide some shelter from the 
elements. Exterior finishes include simulated wood siding, metal accents, standing seam 
metal roofing, and stucco with strategically located control joints. Large storefront 
glazed openings engage an open space plaza provided where sidewalks intersect near 
the roundabout to allow for indoor/outdoor interactions enhancing the pedestrian 
experience and allowing for additional building engagement.”  
33. Utility providers have reviewed the proposed plans and all required utilities are 
available. 
34. Shared solid waste and recycling facilities exist throughout the site.  
35. There are no critical areas impacted as part of the proposal. 
36. The Snyderville Basin General Plan future land use map identifies Quarry Village as 
Mixed-Use Neighborhood Commercial.  
37. Postcard notices announcing the public hearing were mailed to property owners within 
1,000 feet of the subject parcels. 
38. Public notice of the public hearing was published in The Park Record. 
39. Service providers have reviewed the proposed amendment for compliance with 
applicable standards.  
 
Conclusions of Law: 
 
1. The proposed development complies with the applicable standards of the Gorgoza Pines 
Ranch Consent Agreement.  
2. The proposed development complies with the applicable standards of the Quarry 
Junction at Pinebrook Subdivision.  
3. The proposed project, as conditioned, meets the applicable standards set forth in the 
Snyderville Basin Development Code, Section 10-3-5: Conditional Use Permit. 
4. The proposed development complies with the applicable standards of the Snyderville 
Basin Development Code.   
5. The proposed development is consistent with the goals and objectives of the Snyderville 
Basin General Plan. 
 
 
135
11 
 
Conditions of Approval: 
 
1. The locations, heights, and design of the proposed project shall be per Exhibit A of this 
letter. 
2. No outdoor storage is permitted. 
3. Due to lack of delivery space, large-truck deliveries that will block circulation shall be 
prohibited. 
4. Any utilization of large-truck deliveries will require an amendment to this permit to 
accommodate a redesign and incorporation of large-truck deliveries.  
5. Signage and signage relocation approvals are not included as part of this approval and 
shall be reviewed through separate sign permits. 
6. Exterior lighting temperature shall not exceed three thousand degrees (3,000°) Kelvin, 
except the parking lot light pole being replaced which may not exceed five thousand 
degrees (5,000°) Kelvin. 
7. Exterior lighting output shall not exceed two thousand (2,000) Lumens, except the 
parking lot light pole being replaced which may not exceed two thousand five hundred 
(2,500) Lumens. 
8. All disturbed areas shall be revegetated with a native, drought-tolerant seed mix or as 
per the submitted Landscape Plan included in the exhibit package. 
9. A bond shall be in place to ensure site restoration/revegetation of the disturbed areas. 
10. All necessary permits must be obtained, and fees shall be paid prior to the 
commencement of any construction activity, including but not limited to the Summit 
Building Department and Summit County Engineering Department. 
11. All other service provider requirements shall be met. 
12. Business licenses for future uses shall be issued pursuant to the allowed uses identified 
in the Gorgoza Pines Ranch Consent Agreement. 
13. Any business licenses issued that are not according to the uses identified in the Gorgoza 
Pines Ranch Consent Agreement shall be revoked.  
14. Prior to final approval of a building permit, the applicant shall submit construction plans 
to the Engineering Department in conjunction with a Development Improvement 
Agreement. 
15. All mechanical equipment, including equipment related with future tenant 
improvements, shall be screened. 
16. Façade mechanical equipment, including vents, shall be prohibited. 
17. Any façade mechanical equipment, including vents shall require a Low Impact Permit 
Amendment.  
18. No modification to the approved Low Impact Permit is permitted without the prior 
approval of the Summit County Community Development Department.

Subject: Quarry Lot 6 Retail Building – Project Description and Submission  
 
I am writing to provide an overview of the proposed development for Lot 6 of Quarry 
Village Shopping Center, located at 8211 Gorgoza Pines Road, Park City, Utah 84098. 
The project, known as Clover Farms Quarry Development – Lot 6, is a 10,945 SF, two-
story commercial structure designed for retail and/or business occupancy.  No 
restaurants are planned for or anticipated.   
 
The scope of the project includes:  
• Core & shell construction of the building  
• Site improvements and utilities  
• Landscaping and irrigation

grading, drainage, utilities, and building specifications. We are submitting these plans 
for review and approval as part of the permitting process. Please let us know if any 
additional documentation or modifications are required.

233 South Pleasant Grove Blvd. Suite 105, Pleasant Grove, Utah 84062 
Phone (801) 769-3000 • corearch.com 
 
Dear members of the Planning Commission, 
 
We are pleased to present our proposal for the Lot 6  
Quarry Village Development.  This building is designed to be consistent with the 
original community master and is representative of the commitment to high quality 
design, community integration, and long-term neighborhood value. 
 
Project Vision and Intent 
 
The lot 6 building is a two-story, 10,945 square foot structure designed to 
accommodate a flexible mix of professional office and retail tenants.  It is not 
configured to support restaurants, aligning with both parking capacity and community 
vision.  Our intent is to attract good-credit tenants that provide useful services to the 
surrounding Pinebrook, Summit Park, and Jeremy Ranch neighborhoods, augmenting a 
cohesive and vibrant local center. 
 
Architectural Integration 
 
The building design features elements that complement the surrounding buildings 
with their associated scale and character including sloped roof features to provide 
both wayfinding for building entry and provide some shelter from the elements. 
Exterior finishes include simulated wood siding, metal accents, standing seam metal 
roofing, and stucco with strategically located control joints.  Large storefront glazed 
openings engage an open space plaza provided where sidewalks intersect near the 
roundabout to allow for indoor/outdoor interactions enhancing the pedestrian 
experience and allowing for additional building engagement. 
 
Public Spaces and Plazas 
 
A green space plaza is featured between the lot 6 building and the adjacent Kodiak 
Cakes building providing seating, landscaping, and community connectivity.  An open 
space plaza is also provided where sidewalks intersect near the roundabout allowing 
for outdoor interactions while enhancing the pedestrian experience and supporting 
indoor/outdoor engagement. 
 
Functionality and Site Planning 
 
• Snow Storage: Snow storage areas have been incorporated into landscape 
buffer zones along the edges of the property and exceed the 10% minimum 
requirement for uncovered parking areas.   
• Dual Front Design: The primary facades front both Quarry Road to the north 
and Gorgoza Pines Road to the East.  Each façade provides multiple entries.  An 
additional accessible entry to level 2 is provided from the plaza to the south.  
The various entry points allow for street engagement and façade transparency 
and interest as well as flexibility for tenant configurations. 
EXHIBIT A.2
139
 
 
233 South Pleasant Grove Blvd. Suite 105, Pleasant Grove, Utah 84062 
Phone (801) 769-3000 • corearch.com 
 
 
Compliance and Community Alignment 
 
This proposal fully complies with the Gorgoza Pines Consent Agreement and all 
applicable Summit County Development Standards.  Specifically: 
 
• Height: Maximum height is 32’-0” 
• Scale: Designed for small-scale users with a traditional town layout. 
• Parking: Shared parking strategy consistent with the overall Quarry Village 
plan. 
• Trail and Circulation: New sidewalks and stairways improve circulation and 
pedestrian access within the broader development. 
 
We believe this building completes the Quarry Village development in a manner that 
strengthens its sense of place, supports local commercial vitality, and enhances 
neighborhood walkability.  We welcome the opportunity to continue collaborating 
with staff and the Commission, and we respectfully ask for your support of this 
proposal. 
 
Deliveries 
 
The Quarry Village Lot 6 building has been intentionally designed in a way that works 
in concert with the scale and accommodates small-scale retailers, eliminating the 
need for loading docks, overhead doors or large-volume bulk deliveries.  The building 
is not intended to accommodate tenants with heavy delivery needs such as 
warehousing, fulfillment centers, or logistics-driven uses. 
 
Should deliveries be required, they will occur at the front of each unit, utilizing the 
standard parking spaces directly adjacent to the tenant suites.  These short-term 
deliveries can be managed without disruption to daily operations or parking 
availability.  The site design accommodates small delivery vehicles and vans only.

GENERAL NOTES
1.1  COMPLIANCE
1. ALL WORK TO CONFORM TO GOVERNING MUNICIPALITY'S STANDARDS, SPECIFICATIONS AND
REQUIREMENTS.
2. ALL CONSTRUCTION AND MATERIALS SHALL BE IN ACCORDANCE WITH THESE CONTRACT
DOCUMENTS AND THE MOST RECENT, ADOPTED EDITIONS OF THE FOLLOWING: INTERNATIONAL
BUILDING CODE (IBC), THE INTERNATIONAL PLUMBING CODE, STATE DRINKING WATER
REGULATIONS, APWA MANUAL OF STANDARD PLANS AND SPECIFICATIONS, ADA ACCESSIBILITY
GUIDELINES.
3. ALL CONSTRUCTION SHALL BE AS SHOWN ON THESE PLANS. ANY REVISIONS MUST HAVE PRIOR
WRITTEN APPROVAL.
1.2  PERMITTING AND INSPECTIONS
1. PRIOR TO STARTING CONSTRUCTION, THE CONTRACTOR SHALL BE RESPONSIBLE FOR MAKING
SURE THAT ALL REQUIRED PERMITS AND APPROVALS HAVE BEEN OBTAINED. NO
CONSTRUCTION OR FABRICATION SHALL BEGIN UNTIL THE CONTRACTOR HAS RECEIVED
THOROUGHLY REVIEWED PLANS AND OTHER DOCUMENTS APPROVED BY ALL OF THE
PERMITTING AUTHORITIES.
2. CONTRACTOR IS RESPONSIBLE FOR SCHEDULING AND NOTIFYING ARCHITECT/ENGINEER OR
INSPECTING AUTHORITY 48 HOURS IN ADVANCE OF COVERING UP ANY PHASE OF
CONSTRUCTION REQUIRING OBSERVATION.
3. ANY WORK IN THE PUBLIC RIGHT-OF-WAY WILL REQUIRE PERMITS FROM THE APPROPRIATE,
CITY, COUNTY OR STATE AGENCY CONTROLLING THE ROAD AND WITH APPROPRIATE
INSPECTIONS.
1.3  COORDINATION & VERIFICATION
1. ALL DIMENSIONS, GRADES & UTILITY DESIGNS SHOWN ON THE PLANS SHALL BE VERIFIED BY
THE CONTRACTOR PRIOR TO CONSTRUCTION. CONTRACTOR SHALL NOTIFY
ARCHITECT/ENGINEER OF ANY DISCREPANCIES PRIOR TO PROCEEDING WITH CONSTRUCTION
FOR NECESSARY PLAN OR GRADE CHANGES. NO EXTRA COMPENSATION SHALL BE PAID TO THE
CONTRACTOR FOR WORK HAVING TO BE REDONE DUE TO DIMENSIONS OR GRADES SHOWN
INCORRECTLY ON THESE PLANS, IF NOT VERIFIED AND NOTIFICATION OF CONFLICTS HAVE NOT
BEEN BROUGHT TO THE ATTENTION OF THE ARCHITECT/ENGINEER.
2. CONTRACTOR MUST VERIFY ALL EXISTING CONDITIONS BEFORE BIDDING AND BRING UP ANY
QUESTIONS BEFOREHAND. NO ALLOWANCE WILL BE MADE FOR DISCREPANCIES OR OMISSIONS
THAT CAN BE EASILY OBSERVED.
3. CONTRACTOR TO COORDINATE WITH ALL OTHER DISCIPLINES, INCLUDING BUT NOT LIMITED TO:
LANDSCAPE PLANS, SITE ELECTRICAL SITE LIGHTING PLANS AND ELECTRICAL SERVICE TO THE
BUILDING(S), MECHANICAL PLANS FOR LOCATION OF SERVICES TO THE BUILDING(S), INCLUDING
FIRE PROTECTION, ARCHITECTURAL SITE PLAN FOR DIMENSIONS, ACCESSIBLE ROUTES, ETC.,
NOT SHOWN ON CIVIL PLANS.
4. CONTRACTOR IS TO COORDINATE LOCATION OF NEW TELEPHONE SERVICE, GAS SERVICE,
CABLE, ETC. TO BUILDING WITH THE APPROPRIATE UTILITY COMPANY. FOR TELEPHONE,
CONTRACTOR TO FURNISH CONDUIT, PLYWOOD BACKBOARD, AND GROUND WIRE, AS REQUIRED.
1.4  SAFETY AND PROTECTION
1. CONTRACTOR IS SOLELY RESPONSIBLE FOR THE MEANS AND METHODS OF CONSTRUCTION,
2. CONTRACTOR IS RESPONSIBLE FOR THE SAFETY OF THE PROJECT AND SHALL MEET ALL OSHA
REQUIREMENTS.
3. CONTRACTOR IS RESPONSIBLE FOR CONFORMING TO LOCAL AND FEDERAL CODES GOVERNING
SHORING AND BRACING OF EXCAVATIONS AND TRENCHES, AND FOR THE PROTECTION OR
WORKERS AND PUBLIC.
4. CONTRACTOR SHALL TAKE ALL MEASURES NECESSARY TO PROTECT ALL EXISTING PUBLIC AND
PRIVATE PROPERTY, ROADWAYS, AND UTILITY IMPROVEMENTS. DAMAGE TO EXISTING
IMPROVEMENTS CAUSED BY THE CONTRACTOR MUST BE REPAIRED BY THE CONTRACTOR AT
HIS/HER EXPENSE TO THE SATISFACTION OF THE OWNER OF SAID IMPROVEMENTS.
5. CONTRACTOR IS REQUIRED TO KEEP ALL CONSTRUCTION ACTIVITIES WITHIN THE APPROVED
PROJECT LIMITS. THIS INCLUDES, BUT IS NOT LIMITED TO, VEHICLE AND EQUIPMENT STAGING,
MATERIAL STORAGE AND LIMITS OF TRENCH EXCAVATION.
6. IT IS THE CONTRACTOR'S RESPONSIBILITY TO OBTAIN PERMISSION AND/OR EASEMENTS FROM
THE APPROPRIATE GOVERNMENT AGENCY AND/OR INDIVIDUAL PROPERTY OWNER(S) FOR
WORK OR STAGING OUTSIDE OF THE PROJECT LIMITS.
7. CONTRACTOR SHALL PROVIDE BARRICADES, SIGNS, FLASHERS, OTHER EQUIPMENT AND FLAG
PERSONS NECESSARY TO INSURE THE SAFETY OF WORKERS AND VISITORS. ALL
CONSTRUCTION SIGNING, BARRICADING, AND TRAFFIC DELINEATION SHALL CONFORM TO THE
"MANUAL ON UNIFORM TRAFFIC CONTROL DEVICES", LATEST EDITION.
8. CONTRACTOR SHALL COMPLY WITH LOCAL NOISE ORDINANCE STANDARDS.
9. CONTRACTOR IS RESPONSIBLE FOR DUST CONTROL ACCORDING TO GOVERNING AGENCY
STANDARDS.
10. CONTRACTOR SHALL TAKE ALL NECESSARY AND PROPER PRECAUTIONS TO PROTECT
ADJACENT PROPERTIES FROM ANY AND ALL DAMAGE THAT MAY OCCUR FROM STORM WATER
RUNOFF AND/OR DEPOSITION OF DEBRIS RESULTING FROM ANY AND ALL WORK IN CONNECTION
WITH CONSTRUCTION. SUBMIT A STORM WATER POLLUTION PREVENTION PLAN, IF REQUIRED.
11. WORK IN PUBLIC STREETS, ONCE BEGUN, SHALL BE PROSECUTED TO COMPLETION WITHOUT
DELAY AS TO PROVIDE MINIMUM INCONVENIENCE TO ADJACENT PROPERTY OWNERS AND TO
THE TRAVELING PUBLIC.
12. CONTRACTOR SHALL PROVIDE ALL NECESSARY HORIZONTAL AND VERTICAL TRANSITIONS
BETWEEN NEW CONSTRUCTION AND EXISTING SURFACES TO PROVIDE FOR PROPER DRAINAGE
AND FOR INGRESS AND EGRESS TO NEW CONSTRUCTION.
13. NATURAL VEGETATION AND SOIL COVER SHALL NOT BE DISTURBED PRIOR TO ACTUAL
CONSTRUCTION OF A REQUIRED FACILITY OR IMPROVEMENT. MASS CLEARING OF THE SITE IN
ANTICIPATION OF CONSTRUCTION SHALL BE AVOIDED. CONSTRUCTION TRAFFIC SHALL BE
LIMITED TO ONE APPROACH TO THE SITE. THE APPROACH SHALL BE DESIGNATED BY THE
OWNER OR GOVERNING AGENCY.
14. THE CONTRACTOR SHALL TAKE REASONABLE MEASURE TO PROTECT EXISTING IMPROVEMENTS
FROM DAMAGE AND ALL SUCH IMPROVEMENTS DAMAGED BY THE CONTRACTOR'S OPERATION
SHALL BE REPAIRED OR RECONSTRUCTED TO THE ENGINEER/OWNER'S SATISFACTION AT THE
EXPENSE OF THE CONTRACTOR.
1.5  MATERIALS
1. SITE CONCRETE SHALL BE A MINIMUM 6.5 BAG MIX, 4000 P.S.I. @ 28 DAYS, 4" MAXIMUM SLUMP
WITH 5% TO 7% AIR ENTRAINMENT, UNLESS SPECIFIED OTHERWISE. -SEE SPECIFICATION
A. SLABS-ON-GRADE WILL BE TYPICALLY SCORED (1/4 THE DEPTH) AT INTERVALS NOT TO
EXCEED THEIR WIDTH OR 12 TIMES THEIR DEPTH, WHICHEVER IS LESS. SCORING WILL BE
PLACED TO PREVENT RANDOM CRACKING. FULL DEPTH EXPANSION JOINTS WILL BE PLACED
AGAINST ANY OBJECT DEEMED TO BE FIXED, CHANGES IN DIRECTION AND AT EQUAL
INTERVALS NOT TO EXCEED 50 FEET.
B. CONCRETE WATERWAYS, CURBWALLS, MOWSTRIPS, CURB AND GUTTER, ETC. WILL
TYPICALLY BE SCORED (1/4 THE DEPTH AT INTERVALS NOT TO EXCEED 10 FEET AND HAVE
FULL DEPTH EXPANSION JOINTS AT EQUAL SPACING NOT TO EXCEED 50 FEET.
C. UNLESS OTHERWISE NOTED, ALL SLABS-ON-GRADE WILL HAVE A MINIMUM 8" TURNED-DOWN
EDGE TO HELP CONTROL FROST HEAVE.
D. UNLESS OTHERWISE NOTED, ALL ON-GRADE CONCRETE WILL BE PLACED ON A MINIMUM 4"
GRAVEL BASE OVER A WELL COMPACTED (90%) SUBGRADE.
E. ALL EXPOSED SURFACES WILL HAVE A TEXTURED FINISH, RUBBED OR BROOMED. ANY
"PLASTERING" OF NEW CONCRETE WILL BE DONE WHILE IT IS STILL "GREEN".
F. ALL JOINTS (CONTROL, CONSTRUCTION OR EXPANSION JOINTS, ETC.) WILL BE SEALED WITH
A ONE PART POLYURETHANE SEALANT (SEE SPECIFICATION).
2. ASPHALTIC CONCRETE PAVEMENT SHALL BE A MINIMUM 3" OVER 8" OF COMPACTED (95%) ROAD
BASE OVER PROPERLY PREPARED AND COMPACTED (90%) SUBGRADE, UNLESS NOTED
OTHERWISE. -SEE SPECIFICATIONS, AND DETAIL 'D1' SHEET C5.01
A. ASPHALT COMPACTION SHALL BE A MINIMUM 96% (MARSHALL DESIGN).
B. SURFACE COARSE SHALL BE ½ " MINUS. MIX DESIGN TO BE SUBMITTED FOR APPROVAL AT
LEAST TWO WEEKS PRIOR TO ANTICIPATED PAVING SCHEDULE.
C. AC PAVEMENT TO BE A ¼" ABOVE LIP OF ALL GUTTER AFTER COMPACTION.
D. THICKNESSES OVER 3" WILL BE LAID IN TWO LIFTS WITH THE FIRST LIFT BEING AN APPROVED
3/4" MINUS DESIGN.
1.6  GRADING / SOILS
1. SITE GRADING SHALL BE PERFORMED IN ACCORDANCE WITH THESE PLANS AND
SPECIFICATIONS AND THE RECOMMENDATIONS SET FORTH IN THE SOILS REPORT, WHICH BY
REFERENCE ARE A PART OF THE REQUIRED CONSTRUCTION DOCUMENTS AND IN CASE OF
CONFLICT SHALL TAKE PRECEDENCE, UNLESS SPECIFICALLY NOTED OTHERWISE ON THE PLANS,
OR IN THE SPECIFICATIONS. THE CONTRACTOR SHALL NOTIFY THE ENGINEER OF ANY
DISCREPANCY BETWEEN THE SOILS REPORT AND THESE PLANS AND SPECIFICATIONS.
2. THE CONTRACTOR SHALL BE RESPONSIBLE FOR REMOVING AND REPLACING ALL SOFT,
YIELDING OR UNSUITABLE MATERIALS AND REPLACING WITH SUITABLE MATERIALS AS SPECIFIED
IN THE SOILS REPORT.
3. ALL EXCAVATED OR FILLED AREAS SHALL BE COMPACTED TO 95% OF MODIFIED PROCTOR
MAXIMUM DENSITY PER ASTM TEST D-1557, EXCEPT UNDER BUILDING FOUNDATIONS WHERE IT
SHALL BE 98% MIN. OF MAXIMUM DENSITY. MOISTURE CONTENT AT TIME OF PLACEMENT SHALL
NOT EXCEED 2% ABOVE NOR 3% BELOW OPTIMUM.
4. CONTRACTOR SHALL SUBMIT A COMPACTION REPORT PREPARED BY A QUALIFIED REGISTERED
SOILS ENGINEER, VERIFYING THAT ALL FILLED AREAS AND SUBGRADE AREAS WITH THE
BUILDING PAD AREA AND AREAS TO BE PAVED, HAVE BEEN COMPACTED IN ACCORDANCE WITH
THESE PLANS AND SPECIFICATIONS AND THE RECOMMENDATIONS SET FORTH IN THE SOILS
REPORT.
5. SITE CLEARING SHALL INCLUDE THE LOCATING AND REMOVAL OF ALL UNDERGROUND TANKS,
PIPES, VALVES, ETC.
6. ALL EXISTING VALVES, MANHOLES, ETC. SHALL BE RAISED OR LOWERED TO GRADE AS
REQUIRED.
GENERAL NOTES: CONTINUED
1.7  UTILITIES
1. THE LOCATIONS OF UNDERGROUND FACILITIES SHOWN ON THESE PLANS ARE BASED ON FIELD
SURVEYS AND LOCAL UTILITY COMPANY RECORDS. IT SHALL BE THE CONTRACTOR'S FULL
RESPONSIBILITY TO CONTACT THE VARIOUS UTILITY COMPANIES EITHER DIRECT OR THROUGH
BLUE STAKE TO LOCATE THEIR FACILITIES PRIOR TO STARTING CONSTRUCTION.
2. CONTRACTOR TO VERIFY BY POTHOLING BOTH THE VERTICAL AND HORIZONTAL LOCATION OF
ALL EXISTING UTILITIES PRIOR TO INSTALLING ANY NEW LINES. NO ADDITIONAL COMPENSATION
SHALL BE PAID TO THE CONTRACTOR FOR DAMAGE AND REPAIR TO THESE FACILITIES CAUSED
BY HIS WORK FORCE.
3. CONTRACTOR MUST START AT LOW END OF ALL NEW GRAVITY UTILITY LINES. MECHANICAL
SUB-CONTRACTOR MUST BE PROVIDED CIVIL SITE DRAWINGS FOR COORDINATION AND TO
CHECK THE FLOW FROM THE LOWEST POINT IN BUILDING TO THE FIELD VERIFIED CONNECTION
AT THE EXISTING MAIN. NO EXTRA COMPENSATION IS TO BE PAID TO THE CONTRACTOR FOR
WORK HAVING TO BE REDONE DUE TO FAILURE TO COMPLY WITH THESE REQUIREMENTS.
4. CONTRACTOR IS TO VERIFY LOCATION, DEPTH, SIZE, TYPE, AND OUTSIDE DIAMETERS OF
UTILITIES IN THE FIELD BY POTHOLING A MINIMUM OF 300 FEET AHEAD, PIPELINE CONSTRUCTION
TO AVOID CONFLICTS WITH DESIGNED PIPELINE GRADE AND ALIGNMENT. EXISTING UTILITY
INFORMATION SHOWN ON PLANS OR OBTAINED FROM UTILITY COMPANIES OR BLUE STAKED
MUST BE ASSUMED AS APPROXIMATE, REQUIRING FIELD VERIFICATION.
5. CULINARY WATER AND FIRE SERVICE LINES TO BE CONSTRUCTED IN ACCORDANCE WITH LOCAL
GOVERNING MUNICIPALITY STANDARDS AND SPECIFICATIONS.
6. SANITARY SEWER MAINS AND LATERALS TO BE CONSTRUCTED IN ACCORDANCE WITH LOCAL
GOVERNING MUNICIPALITY SEWER DISTRICT STANDARDS AND SPECIFICATIONS.
7. STORM SEWER TO BE CONSTRUCTED IN ACCORDANCE WITH THE GOVERNING MUNICIPALITY
STANDARDS AND SPECIFICATIONS.
8. ALL STORM DRAIN AND IRRIGATION CONDUITS SHALL BE INSTALLED WITH WATER TIGHT JOINTS
AND CONNECTIONS.
9. ALL STORM DRAIN PIPE PENETRATIONS INTO BOXES SHALL BE CONSTRUCTED WITH WATER
TIGHT SEALS ON THE OUTSIDE AND GROUTED SMOOTH WITH A NON-SHRINK GROUT ON THE
INSIDE. CONDUITS SHALL BE CUT OFF FLUSH WITH THE INSIDE OF THE BOX.
10. NO CHANGE IN THE DESIGN OF UTILITIES AS SHOWN WILL BE MADE BY THE CONTRACTOR
WITHOUT THE WRITTEN APPROVAL OF THE GOVERNING MUNICIPALITY, OR OTHER AUTHORITY
HAVING JURISDICTION OVER THAT UTILITY.
11. ALL STORM DRAIN CONDUITS AND BOXES SHALL BE CLEAN AND FREE OF ROCKS, DIRT, AND
CONSTRUCTION DEBRIS PRIOR TO FINAL INSPECTION.
1.8  SURVEY CONTROL
1. CONTRACTOR MUST PROVIDE A REGISTERED LAND SURVEYOR OR PERSONS UNDER THE
SUPERVISION OF A REGISTERED LAND SURVEYOR TO SET STAKES FOR THE ALIGNMENT AND
GRADE OF EACH MAIN AND/OR FACILITY AS SHOWN ON THE PLANS. THE STAKES SHALL BE
MARKED WITH THE HORIZONTAL LOCATION (STATION) AND VERTICAL LOCATION (GRADE) WITH
CUTS AND/OR FILLS TO THE APPROVED GRADE OF THE MAIN AND OR FACILITY AS SHOWN ON
THE PLANS.
2. THE CONTRACTOR SHALL PROTECT ALL STAKES AND MARKERS FOR VERIFICATION PURPOSES.
3. CONTRACTOR WILL BE RESPONSIBLE FOR FURNISHING, MAINTAINING, OR RESTORING ALL
MONUMENTS AND REFERENCE MARKS WITHIN THE PROJECT SITE.
1.9  AMERICAN DISABILITIES ACT
1. PEDESTRIAN / ADA ROUTES SHALL MEET THE FOLLOWING SPECIFICATIONS:
*ROUTES SHALL HAVE A 2.08% (1:48) MAXIMUM CROSS SLOPE.
*ROUTES SHALL HAVE A 5.00% (1:20) MAXIMUM RUNNING SLOPE.
*RAMPS SHALL HAVE A 8.33% (1:12) MAXIMUM RUNNING SLOPE.
2. ADA PARKING STALLS AND ADJACENT ROUTES SHALL HAVE A 2.08% (1:48) MAXIMUM SURFACE
SLOPE IN ANY DIRECTION.
3. THE CONTRACTOR SHALL ADHERE TO THE ABOVE SPECIFICATIONS. IN THE EVENT OF A
DISCREPANCY IN THE CONSTRUCTION DOCUMENTS, THE CONTRACTOR SHALL NOTIFY THE
ARCHITECT/ENGINEER PRIOR TO ANY CONSTRUCTION.
